<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct a language in deterministic polynomial time whose <i>n</i>-bit membership function requires $`2^{\omega(\sqrt n)}`$ gates in an unbounded-fan-in OR–AND–OR circuit. The bound holds at every sufficiently large input length and counts all gates, including the bottom layer.

- **c1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is a language $L\subseteq\{0,1\}^*$, a deterministic Turing machine deciding $L$, and fixed positive integers $C,a$ such that the machine runs in time at most $C(n+1)^a$ on every input of length $n$. If $f_n$ is the membership function of $L$ on $\{0,1\}^n$, then $$\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\log_2 S_3(f_n)}{\sqrt n}=\infty.$$ Equivalently, for every real $A>0$, there is an integer $N_A$ such that $$S_3(f_n)>2^{A\sqrt n}\qquad\text{for every }n\ge N_A.$$ No uniformity or bottom fan-in restriction is imposed on the circuits.
  - Explanation: The same polynomial-time language exceeds every fixed square-root exponent in total depth-three gate complexity at all sufficiently large lengths.
- **c2** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-restriction)): Let $k\geq0$ and $b\geq1$ be integers, let $H$ be a CNF of width at most $k$ on $[d]$, and let $0<p<1$. Choose $T\subseteq[d]$ by retaining each coordinate independently with probability $p$, and choose a uniform assignment $\rho$ outside $T$. Suppose $$\label{eq:restriction-theta} \theta=\sum_{\ell=b+1}^{k}\binom{k}{\ell} \left(\frac{2p}{1-p}\right)^{\ell}<1.$$ There are numbers $W(H,T,\rho)\geq 0$, depending only on the formula and the restriction, such that $$\label{eq:restriction-cost} \mathbb E_{T,\rho}W(H,T,\rho)\leq\frac{1}{1-\theta}$$ and, simultaneously for every real-valued function $G$ on $\{0,1\}^T$, $$\label{eq:restriction-correlation} \left\|\mathbb E_z G(z)H_{T,\rho}(z)\right\| \leq W(H,T,\rho)\,\mathop{\mathrm{corr}}_b(G).$$ An empty sum in <a href="#eq:restriction-theta" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:restriction-theta">[eq:restriction-theta]</a> is zero.
  - Explanation: Random restriction transfers correlation to bounded-width CNF tests through an expected coefficient cost independent of clause count.
- **c3** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sparse-partition)): For every integer $b\ge 1$ and real $\eta>0$, there is a positive integer $M=M(b,\eta)$ with the following property. Every CNF indicator $H$ of width at most $b$ on $m\ge 1$ variables can be written pointwise as $$H=\sum_{a=1}^{q} H_a, \qquad q\le 2^{\eta m},$$ where the $H_a$ are CNF indicators of width at most $b$, each represented by at most $Mm$ clauses, and their satisfying sets are pairwise disjoint. The empty sum is permitted when $H=0$.
  - Explanation: A bounded-width CNF partitions into disjoint sparse CNF indicators while preserving its width.
- **c4** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sunflower-bound)): Let $s\ge 0$ and $r\ge 2$ be integers. A family of more than $s!(r-1)^s$ distinct sets of cardinality $s$ contains a sunflower with $r$ members.
  - Explanation: A sufficiently large uniform set family contains a sunflower.
- **c5** (construction, [source](#stmt-def-language)): For an input of length $n$, put $d=\left\lfloor n/5\right\rfloor$ and reject if $d<64$. Otherwise define $$r=\left\lceil d^{2/3}\right\rceil,\qquad t=\max\{j\in\mathbb N\cup\{0\}: j\text{ is even and }j^6\le d\}.$$ Reject if fewer than $2d+(t+2)r-1$ input bits are available. Otherwise parse the input into the following consecutive blocks: 1. $d$ data bits $x=(x_1,\ldots,x_d)$; 2. $d+r-1$ hash bits $u_1,\ldots,u_{d+r-1}$; 3. $r$ bits $p_0,\ldots,p_{r-1}$, encoding the monic polynomial $$P(Z)=Z^r+\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}p_iZ^i\in\mathbb F_2[Z];$$ 4. $t$ blocks of $r$ bits, encoding elements $\beta_0,\ldots,\beta_{t-1}$ of the quotient ring $R_P=\mathbb F_2[Z]/(P)$, in the basis represented by $1,Z,\ldots,Z^{r-1}$. Unused bits are ignored. Form $$\label{eq:toeplitz-hash} h_i(x)=\sum_{s=1}^d u_{i+s-1}x_s\pmod2 \quad(1\le i\le r),\qquad h(x)=\sum_{i=1}^r h_i(x)Z^{i-1}\in R_P.$$ Let $\lambda:R_P\to\mathbb F_2$ take the degree-zero coefficient of the unique representative of degree less than $r$. The input belongs to $L$ exactly when $$\label{eq:language-evaluation} \lambda\left(\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}\beta_jh(x)^j\right)=0.$$
  - Explanation: Membership parses data, hash, modulus and polynomial-coefficient blocks and evaluates in the supplied quotient ring.
- **c6** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-prop-polytime)): The language $L$ is decided on every input of length $n$ by one deterministic Turing machine in time $C(n+1)^a$, for some fixed positive integers $C,a$.
  - Explanation: A fixed deterministic evaluator computes membership in polynomial time without finding hard parameters or testing irreducibility.
- **c7** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-good-slices)): For each fixed integer $b\ge1$ and real $B>0$, all sufficiently large lengths $n$ admit fixed settings of the non-data bits such that, with $$p=\frac{B}{\sqrt d},\qquad m_0=pd=B\sqrt d,$$ the resulting sign $g$ satisfies $$\label{eq:good-slices} \Pr_{T,\rho}\left[ \|T\|\notin[m_0/2,2m_0] \ \text{or}\ \mathop{\mathrm{corr}}_b(g_{T,\rho})>2^{-\|T\|/8} \right] \le4\cdot2^{-m_0/16}.$$ Here $T$ includes each of the $d$ data coordinates independently with probability $p$, and $\rho$ is an independent uniform assignment outside $T$. In particular, the correlation bound on a good restriction holds simultaneously for all members of $\mathcal C_b(T)$.
  - Explanation: For fixed width and restriction scale, some non-data settings give simultaneous small correlation on most random restrictions.
- **c8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-field-existence)): For every integer $r\ge1$, there is a monic irreducible polynomial of degree $r$ over $\mathbb F_2$.
  - Explanation: An irreducible monic binary polynomial exists in every positive degree.

Such a circuit is a disjunction of CNFs: conjunctions of clauses, each a disjunction of literals. Classical lower bounds for explicit functions have an exponent proportional to the square root of the input length. Every bottom gate and the output gate are counted. Input negations are free. The three layers are successive: bottom gates read literals and optional constants, middle gates read bottom outputs, and the top gate reads middle outputs.           "exact_quote": "Let $k\\geq0$ and $b\\geq1$ be integers, let $H$ be a CNF of width at most $k$ on $[d]$, and let $0<p<1$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Total circuit size** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Throughout the paper, $S_3(f)$ denotes the minimum *total* number of AND and OR gates in an OR–AND–OR circuit computing $f$ exactly.
- **Clause** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): A *clause* is an OR of literals, and a *CNF* is an AND of clauses.
- **Clause scope and width** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): Its *scope* is the set of these variables, and its *width* is the size of its scope.
- **Empty CNF** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): The empty CNF is the constant-true function.
- **Bounded-width CNF family** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): For an integer $b\ge1$, let $\mathcal C_b(V)$ be the set of CNF indicators on $\{0,1\}^V$ with clause widths at most $b$.
- **Maximum bounded-width correlation** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): For $G:\{0,1\}^V\to\mathbb R$, define $$\label{eq:correlation-definition} \mathop{\mathrm{corr}}_b(G)=\max_{J\in\mathcal C_b(V)} \left\|\mathbb E_{z\in\{0,1\}^V}G(z)J(z)\right\|.$$
- **Random restriction** ([source](#eq:restriction-expectation)): A $p$-random restriction of $[d]=\{1,\ldots,d\}$, for $0<p<1$, is sampled in two stages. Include each coordinate independently in a live set $T$ with probability $p$; then choose an independent uniform assignment $\rho$ to $[d]\setminus T$.
- **Sunflower** ([source](#sec:sparse)): A *sunflower* is a collection of distinct sets whose pairwise intersections all equal a fixed set $C$, called its *core*.
- **Data dimension** ([source](#stmt-def-language)): For an input of length $n$, put $d=\left\lfloor n/5\right\rfloor$ and reject if $d<64$.
- **Coefficient projection** ([source](#stmt-def-language)): Let $\lambda:R_P\to\mathbb F_2$ take the degree-zero coefficient of the unique representative of degree less than $r$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **r1** [claim](#stmt-lem-restriction) / [proof](#sec:restriction): Finite signed expansion and reverse path counting -> Expected coefficient mass independent of clause count
- **r2** [claim](#stmt-lem-sparse-partition) / [proof](#sec:sparse): Sunflower branching with disjoint side conditions -> Disjoint bounded-width sparse pieces
- **r3** [claim](#stmt-prop-polytime) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-polytime): Integer parameter computation and quotient-ring Horner evaluation -> A fixed polynomial-time membership evaluator
- **r4** [claim](#stmt-lem-field-existence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-field-existence): Splitting-field and minimal-polynomial argument -> Existence of a monic irreducible binary polynomial
- **r5** [claim](#stmt-prop-good-slices) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-good-slices): Injective hashing, interpolation, moment bounds and averaging -> A fixed slice with simultaneous correlation control
- **r6** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#sec:lower-bound): Correlation transfer, clause truncation and acceptance union bound -> The total-gate lower bound at all sufficiently large lengths

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **ring-evaluation**: Monic moduli give well-defined quotient-ring polynomial evaluation even when the modulus is reducible.
- **fixed-language**: The membership machine is fixed while the circuit lower-bound exponent can be chosen arbitrarily.
- **hard-slices**: Hard slices are obtained by fixing parameter bits; membership evaluation does not have to find those settings.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **find-hard-slice**: Use membership evaluation as an algorithm to find hard parameter settings. -> Evaluation uses supplied bits; the existence argument does not supply a search algorithm.
- **count-middle-only**: Measure arbitrary-bottom-width circuit complexity solely by the number of middle CNFs. -> A single CNF can represent any Boolean function while hiding its bottom-gate cost.
- **finite-proof**: Treat agreement on finite ring evaluations as a certificate of the circuit lower bound. -> Finite arithmetic agreement does not establish the asymptotic quantifiers or the correlation argument.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The finite polynomial evaluation in the supplied quotient ring is constructive; this harness checks that arithmetic subroutine and does not certify the asymptotic lower bound.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of [r, modulus_lower_bits, coefficients, hash_value].
# PRECONDITIONS: r positive; modulus monic; all encoded residues in range.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of degree-zero evaluation coefficients in F_2.
# TIME: O(sum(t*r^2)) bit operations for the admitted evaluations.
# SPACE: O(sum(t*r)) input bits and O(r) arithmetic workspace per case.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact binary polynomial arithmetic; no floating point.
# FAILURE: Invalid contract raises an exception and exits nonzero.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpz

def product(a, b, r, low):
    z = 0
    for i in range(r):
        if (b >> i) & 1:
            z ^= a << i
    modulus = (1 << r) | low
    for degree in range(2*r-2, r-1, -1):
        if (z >> degree) & 1:
            z ^= modulus << (degree-r)
    return z

def evaluate(case):
    r, low, coeff, h = case
    assert type(r) is int and r >= 1
    assert all(type(v) is int and 0 <= v < (1 << r) for v in [low, h] + coeff)
    value = 0
    for beta in reversed(coeff):
        value = product(value, h, r, low) ^ beta
    return int(fmpz(value) & 1)

print(json.dumps([evaluate(c) for c in json.loads(sys.stdin.read())], separators=(',', ':')))
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |

| software | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The paper supplies a fixed deterministic membership algorithm with finite bit-array operations and polynomial storage. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A game or simulation contract is ABSENT; the object computed is the membership function of a language. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The exact circuit model can be used to specify a circuit-analysis contract, provided the analyzed circuit computes the stated membership function exactly. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | inferred | Linear hashing, quotient-ring reduction and Horner evaluation give a finite algorithm for the defined membership predicate. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | stated | Coefficient arrays represent quotient-ring elements and support the stated direct multiplication and reduction procedure. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | An accelerator implementation or performance contract is ABSENT; the lower bound concerns exact Boolean OR–AND–OR circuits and total gate count. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The hash and polynomial family support a correlation argument; a cryptographic security contract is ABSENT. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A learning algorithm, sample-complexity result or prediction guarantee is ABSENT; correlation is studied against bounded-width CNF indicators. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | An optimisation or operations-research contract is ABSENT; the circuit argument proves a lower bound for exact computation of the defined language. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A distributed protocol, communication model or fault-tolerance contract is ABSENT; the evaluator is a fixed deterministic Turing machine. |
| other (Boolean circuit complexity) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The main result directly concerns Boolean circuit complexity of a fixed polynomial-time language. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **l1** (formal_scope): The lower bound concerns exact computation in the successive OR–AND–OR model with total AND and OR gates counted, including bottom gates and the output gate; input negations are free. Blocked use: ['Using only the number of middle CNFs as the complexity measure.', 'Transferring the bound to another gate basis, circuit depth, runtime, or silicon area without an additional argument.']
- **l2** (hypothesis): The language and its polynomial-time machine are fixed before the desired lower-bound constant is chosen; the length threshold can depend on that constant. Blocked use: ['Treating the theorem as a numerical gate lower bound at a specified finite input length without establishing the corresponding threshold.']
- **l3** (counterexample_scope): The result makes the square-root exponent ratio unbounded but does not establish a fixed power improvement of the exponent. Blocked use: ['Claiming a lower bound with a fixed positive power improvement over the square-root exponent.']
- **l4** (counterexample_scope): Parity does not supply the improvement sought by this construction. Blocked use: ['Attributing the constructed language’s improved lower bound to parity.']
- **l5** (nonconstructive): Hard-slice parameter settings are supplied by probabilistic existence; deciding membership uses the supplied bits and does not find a hard slice or test irreducibility. Blocked use: ['Claiming an algorithm that produces hard parameter settings from the membership evaluator.', 'Treating arbitrary supplied parameters as certified hard slices.']
- **l6** (hypothesis): The hard-slice choices can vary with the fixed width, restriction scale, and input length; these proof parameters must be fixed before taking the length to infinity. Blocked use: ['Using the hard-slice proposition with width or restriction scale growing with input length without a new proof.']
- **l7** (hypothesis): The restriction estimate assumes independent live-coordinate choices and an independent uniform assignment outside the live set, with the displayed theta strictly less than one. Blocked use: ['Applying the expected coefficient-mass bound to correlated or nonuniform restrictions.', 'Using the geometric bound when its strict theta precondition fails.']
- **l8** (hypothesis): The sparse partition has fixed bounded width, positive sparsification tolerance, and a positive number of variables; its clause-count constant depends on width and tolerance. Blocked use: ['Treating the clause-count constant as independent of width or sparsification tolerance.']
- **l9** (edge_case): Small inputs are rejected by the fixed language definition; the evaluator also checks the availability of all parameter blocks and ignores unused bits. Blocked use: ['Skipping the rejection or block-boundary checks when implementing membership.', 'Treating unused bits as additional data coordinates.']
- **l10** (formal_scope): Every monic modulus defines the evaluator’s quotient ring, while the hardness analysis selects an irreducible modulus to obtain a field. Blocked use: ['Using field inverses or interpolation for arbitrary monic modulus blocks.', 'Rejecting reducible modulus blocks as part of the language definition.']
- **l11** (approximation): The theorem concerns exact circuit computation. The proof’s deletion of wide clauses introduces a bounded intermediate error, rather than a theorem for approximate circuits. Blocked use: ['Claiming the same lower bound for approximate computation from the truncation estimate alone.']
- **l12** (formal_scope): Finite exact tests of the language evaluator can check membership calculations but do not certify the asymptotic gate lower bound. Blocked use: ['Publishing a finite evaluator self-test as verification of the asymptotic circuit theorem.']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 112 (`lean/docs/112.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Beyond the Square-Root Exponent for Depth-Three Boolean Circuits](../../preprints/Beyond-the-Square-Root-Exponent-for-Depth-Three-Boolean-Circuits-September-23-2026/main.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalized result gives one polynomial-time Boolean language whose exact depth-three OR–AND–OR circuit size eventually exceeds $2^{A\sqrt n}$ for every fixed $A>0$. The language and its polynomial-time algorithm are fixed before $A$ is chosen, while circuits may vary with the input length. Gates have unrestricted finite fan-in and sharing, with free input negations and constants. The result does not assert a fixed exponent $n^{1/2+\varepsilon}$ or a lower bound for unrestricted depth.

Comparator source `DepthThree.lean` (4453 bytes, sha256 `c12686c38119eb50c375790ebc28e0c806821714985431e59f18ae87649872a4`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace DepthThreeLowerBound

universe uV uS uK uGamma uQ

abbrev Cube (V : Type uV) := V → Bool
abbrev Literal (V : Type uV) := V × Bool

variable {V : Type uV}

namespace Literal

def eval (l : Literal V) (x : Cube V) : Bool := decide (x l.1 = l.2)

end Literal

abbrev GateInput (V : Type uV) := Sum (Literal V) Bool
abbrev RawClause (V : Type uV) := List (GateInput V)

namespace GateInput

def eval : GateInput V → Cube V → Bool
  | .inl l, x => l.eval x
  | .inr b, _ => b

end GateInput

namespace RawClause

noncomputable def eval (C : RawClause V) (x : Cube V) : Bool := by
  classical
  exact decide (∃ l ∈ C, l.eval x = true)

end RawClause

structure Circuit3 (V : Type uV) where
  bottomCount : ℕ
  middleCount : ℕ
  bottom : Fin bottomCount → RawClause V
  middle : Fin middleCount → Finset (Fin bottomCount)
  top : Finset (Fin middleCount)

namespace Circuit3

def gateCount (C : Circuit3 V) : ℕ := C.bottomCount + C.middleCount + 1

noncomputable def middleEval (C : Circuit3 V) (j : Fin C.middleCount)
    (x : Cube V) : Bool := by
  classical
  exact decide (∀ i ∈ C.middle j, (C.bottom i).eval x = true)

noncomputable def eval (C : Circuit3 V) (x : Cube V) : Bool := by
  classical
  exact decide (∃ j ∈ C.top, C.middleEval j x = true)

def Computes (C : Circuit3 V) (f : Cube V → Bool) : Prop := ∀ x, C.eval x = f x

end Circuit3

def runSteps {S : Type uS} (step : S → Option S) (n : ℕ) (c : Option S) : Option S :=
  (fun oc => oc.bind step)^[n] c

inductive HeadMove
  | left
  | stay
  | right

def HeadMove.apply {Γ : Type uGamma} [Inhabited Γ] : HeadMove → Turing.Tape Γ → Turing.Tape Γ
  | .left, t => t.move Turing.Dir.left
  | .stay, t => t
  | .right, t => t.move Turing.Dir.right

structure MultiTapeCfg (K : Type uK) (Γ : Type uGamma) (Q : Type uQ) [Inhabited Γ] where
  q : Q
  tapes : K → Turing.Tape Γ

abbrev MultiTapeCode (K : Type uK) (Γ : Type uGamma) (Q : Type uQ) :=
  Q → (K → Γ) → Option (Q × (K → Γ) × (K → HeadMove))

def multiTapeUpdate {K : Type uK} {Γ : Type uGamma} {Q : Type uQ} [Inhabited Γ] (c : MultiTapeCfg K Γ Q)
    (q : Q) (writes : K → Γ) (moves : K → HeadMove) : MultiTapeCfg K Γ Q :=
  ⟨q, fun k => (moves k).apply ((c.tapes k).write (writes k))⟩

def multiTapeStep {K : Type uK} {Γ : Type uGamma} {Q : Type uQ} [Inhabited Γ] (code : MultiTapeCode K Γ Q)
    (c : MultiTapeCfg K Γ Q) : Option (MultiTapeCfg K Γ Q) :=
  (code c.q (fun k => (c.tapes k).head)).map fun out =>
    multiTapeUpdate c out.1 out.2.1 out.2.2

structure FiniteMultiTapeMachine where
  K : Type
  [tapeFinite : Fintype K]
  [tapeDecidableEq : DecidableEq K]
  inputTape : K
  Γ : Type
  [alphabetInhabited : Inhabited Γ]
  [alphabetFinite : Fintype Γ]
  Q : Type
  [stateFinite : Fintype Q]
  initialState : Q
  inputSymbol : Bool → Γ
  input_injective : Function.Injective inputSymbol
  input_ne_blank : ∀ b, inputSymbol b ≠ default
  code : MultiTapeCode K Γ Q
  accept : Q → Bool

attribute [instance] FiniteMultiTapeMachine.tapeFinite
  FiniteMultiTapeMachine.tapeDecidableEq FiniteMultiTapeMachine.alphabetInhabited
  FiniteMultiTapeMachine.alphabetFinite FiniteMultiTapeMachine.stateFinite

namespace FiniteMultiTapeMachine

abbrev Cfg (M : FiniteMultiTapeMachine) := MultiTapeCfg M.K M.Γ M.Q

abbrev step (M : FiniteMultiTapeMachine) : M.Cfg → Option M.Cfg :=
  multiTapeStep M.code

def init (M : FiniteMultiTapeMachine) (input : List Bool) : M.Cfg :=
  ⟨M.initialState, fun k =>
    if k = M.inputTape then Turing.Tape.mk₁ (input.map M.inputSymbol)
    else Turing.Tape.mk₁ []⟩

end FiniteMultiTapeMachine

def MultiTapeHaltsIn (M : FiniteMultiTapeMachine) (input : List Bool)
    (output : Bool) (time : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∃ k ≤ time, ∃ c : M.Cfg,
    runSteps M.step k (some (M.init input)) = some c ∧
    M.step c = none ∧ M.accept c.q = output

theorem exists_polynomial_time_language_depth_three_lower_bound :
    ∃ (L : List Bool → Bool) (M : FiniteMultiTapeMachine) (C a : ℕ),
      0 < C ∧ 0 < a ∧
      (∀ w : List Bool, MultiTapeHaltsIn M w (L w) (C * (w.length + 1) ^ a)) ∧
      ∀ A : ℝ, 0 < A → ∃ N : ℕ, ∀ n : ℕ, N ≤ n →
        ∀ D : Circuit3 (Fin n), D.Computes (fun x => L (List.ofFn x)) →
          (2 : ℝ) ^ (A * Real.sqrt (n : ℝ)) < (D.gateCount : ℝ) := by
  sorry

end DepthThreeLowerBound

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct a language in deterministic polynomial time whose <i>n</i>-bit membership function requires $`2^{\omega(\sqrt n)}`$ gates in an unbounded-fan-in OR–AND–OR circuit. The bound holds at every sufficiently large input length and counts all gates, including the bottom layer.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index
